The participant reported no prior medical history and did not take any concomitant medications. Treatment began on (b) (6) with DRSP/E4 3/14.2 mg for 10
L1398: ## Nextstellis (drospirenone and estetrol tablets)
L1399: days, followed by 15/71 mg for another 10 days. The participant received study drug for a total of 20 days and completed treatment on [REDACTED](b)(6). The participant was diagnosed with deep vein thrombosis on [REDACTED](b)(6) three days after treatment completion.
L1400: On [REDACTED](b)(6) the participant experienced pain and mild swelling of her right lower leg and behind her right knee after travelling home from the clinic site seated in a car for several hours. She did not experience shortness of breath. The participant reported these symptoms to the study site during a follow-up call to inform the participant of the low platelet count on [REDACTED](b)(6). The participant went to the nearest Emergency Department for evaluation of possible DVT as per study site instructions. An ultrasound vein duplex of the lower right extremity was performed.
L1401: On [REDACTED](b)(6) the participant was diagnosed with deep vein thrombosis: ultrasound revealed an occlusive thrombus of the right proximal to distal superficial femoral vein and a non-occlusive thrombus within the right popliteal vein. The participant was prescribed apixaban 10 mg for 7 days followed by 5 mg twice daily for 21 days. The participant was discharged from the Emergency Department with instructions to follow-up with her primary care provider in one week.
L1402: On [REDACTED](b)(6) the participant reported the pain was resolving and swelling of the affected areas had decreased. The participant had completed study treatment at the time of the event. The adverse event of deep vein thrombosis was assessed as likely related to study drug. The participant was tested for Factor V Leiden and Prothrombin 20210GA mutations; both results were negative. The low platelet count of 81 thousand/\muL on [REDACTED](b)(6) was not categorized as an AE or SAE.

L1406: The Review Division concludes that the adverse event of deep vein thrombosis is likely related to study drug. L1407: ## **Complicated Migraine (Case #6)**
L1408: **Participant** [REDACTED](b)(6) was a 24-year-old Caucasian premenopausal female who enrolled in Study MIT-Es0001-C106 – a randomized, double-blind parallel group with nested cross-over study to evaluate the pharmacokinetics and investigate the effects of therapeutic and supratherapeutic
L1409: ## Nextstellis (drospirenone and estetrol tablets)
L1410: concentrations of DRSP/E4 on heart rate corrected QT interval (QTc). Relevant medical history included Lyme disease, menstrual pain and allergies to codeine and penicillin; she had no prior history of migraines. Concomitant medications at the time of the adverse event included ibuprofen. The participant reported a prior history of contraceptive pill use without adverse effects.
L1411: The participant began study treatment on [REDACTED](b)(6) The participant received 1 dose of moxifloxacin-placebo on [REDACTED](b)(6) (Day 1), followed by 10 days of DRSP 3 mg/E4 14.2 mg, and subsequently 10 days DRSP 15 mg/E4 71 mg (5x therapeutic dose) per protocol. The participant took the last dose of study treatment on [REDACTED](b)(6) (Day 20).
L1412: On [REDACTED](b)(6) 19 days after first study product dose, the participant experienced visual disturbances, hyperventilation, and difficulty communicating. She subsequently developed tingling of the right side of the mouth, right hand and arm, and frontal headache, and became less responsive. The participant was sent to the local hospital for further evaluation. Upon arrival at the Emergency Department, she was noted to be lethargic but arousable and able to cooperate with examination. Neurological examination revealed no significant findings.
L1413: The participant was admitted to the hospital for pain management and further evaluation. Computed tomography (CT) of the brain without contrast, computed tomography angiography (CTA) of the head and neck with IV contrast, magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) of the brain without contrast and magnetic resonance venography (MRV) of the head without contrast were performed and revealed no abnormalities. An echocardiogram showed a small right-to-left shunt at rest and with Valsalva. Laboratory tests were within normal limits. There was no evidence of infection. The participant received ketorolac, ondansetron, and acetylsalicylic acid (81 mg). The participant's symptoms resolved on the day of admission. She was discharged home the following day, [REDACTED](b)(6)
L1414: The participant did not complete the study due to the adverse event and therefore did not receive moxifloxacin-placebo on Day 21 as per protocol.
L1415: The Investigator and Sponsor classified the unexpected adverse event of “complicated migraine” as possibly related to the study medication. The Investigator and Sponsor assessed the event as possibly related to moxifloxacin.
L1416: The Review Division concludes that the adverse event of “complicated migraine” is likely related to study product given that the event occurred after receiving supra-therapeutic doses of DRSP/E4 and had no prior history of migraine.
L1417: The Review Division disagrees that the adverse event of “complicated migraine” is possibly related to moxifloxacin. The prescribing information for moxifloxacin does not indicate that migraine is an expected adverse reaction and it is a known adverse event related to both estrogen and combined hormonal contraceptive use. Additionally, there are no reports in the literature for migraine associated with moxifloxacin. Therefore, the Review Division considers this event unlikely related to moxifloxacin and related to use of NEXTSTELLIS.

L1424: ## PHARMACOLOGY/TOXICOLOGY
## SECONDARY REVIEW
L1425: NDA #:	214154 (IND 110682)
Supporting Document #:	SDN #1; eCTD 0001
Received Date:	April 15, 2020
PDUFA Goal Date:	April 15, 2021
Sponsor:	Mayne Pharma LLC
Drug:	Drospirenone/Estetrol (DRSP/E4)
Proposed Tradename:	NEXTSTELLIS
Pharmacologic Class:	Progestin / Estrogen
Indication:	Prevention of pregnancy
Secondary Reviewer:	Kimberly Hatfield, PhD
Tertiary Reviewer:	Mukesh Summan, PhD, DABT
Primary Reviewer:	Andrea Benedict, PhD
Pharm/Tox Division:	Division of Pharm/Tox for Rare Diseases, Pediatrics, Urologic and Reproductive Medicine / Specialty Medicine (DPT-RPURM/SM)
Clinical Review Division:	Division of Urology, Obstetrics and Gynecology (DUOG)
Recommendation on Regulatory Action:	Approval
Review Date:	April 12, 2021
L1426: I agree with the nonclinical reviewer that there are no nonclinical concerns for the safety of this drospirenone/estetrol (DRSP/E4) combined hormonal contraceptive (CHC) product, and that Pharmacology/Toxicology supports the approval of NEXTSTELLIS for prevention of pregnancy in females under NDA 214154.
L1427: E4 is a New Molecular Entity (NME), and a full nonclinical battery of studies were completed to support the nonclinical safety of E4 as the estrogen component of this CHC. E4 shows selective estrogen receptor agonist activity in vitro, but is less potent than estrogens commonly used in CHCs such as ethinyl estradiol (EE) and estradiol (E2). E4 also has the same pharmacology as related estrogens and inhibits ovulation in animal models. Toxicology studies in rats and monkeys showed expected exaggerated pharmacologic effects of an estrogen, and both carcinogenicity and reproductive toxicity studies with E4 alone demonstrated consistent estrogen class effects with no unanticipated toxicities. A 13-week study in monkeys dosed with a combination of E4
L1428: and DRSP also showed exaggerated pharmacologic effects of a progestin/estrogen combination, with no unanticipated toxicities.
L1429: DRSP is an approved progestin component of contraceptive products, both alone and in combination with estrogens. To support the nonclinical safety of DRSP in NEXTSTELLIS, the applicant conducted a relative bioavailability study to establish an adequate scientific bridge to the DRSP component in the approved product YAZ, and therefore is able to rely on the Agency's previous findings of safety for DRSP in YAZ as reflected in the product labeling. The nonclinical sections of labeling for NEXTSTELLIS will align with the DRSP information as represented in the YAZ approved drug product labeling.
L1430: In conclusion, the nonclinical profile of E4 administered alone or in combination with DRSP is consistent with that of other estrogenic compounds or CHC estrogen/progestin products. There are no anticipated concerns for use of NEXTSTELLIS when used clinically in females of reproductive potential to prevent pregnancy.

L1438: ## Introduction
L1439: NEXTSTELLIS is a proposed fixed-dose combined hormonal contraceptive (CHC) drug product for the prevention of pregnancy in women that contains a novel estrogen, estetrol (E4; 14.2 mg [anhydrous]), and a progestin, drospirenone (DRSP; 3 mg). The E4 component is a New Molecular Entity (NME), and as such, a full battery of nonclinical pharmacology, pharmacokinetic, and toxicology studies have been completed to support the nonclinical safety of this drug product component. The Applicant refers to the Agency's previous findings of safety for the nonclinical pharmacology, pharmacokinetics, and toxicology of the DRSP component of the approved product YAZ® (NDA 021676, approved March 16, 2006) to support the nonclinical safety of drospirenone in NEXTSTELLIS. YAZ® is an approved CHC composed of DRSP (3 mg) and ethinyl estradiol (0.02 mg) and is appropriate for the applicant to reference as a listed drug.
L1440: The nonclinical review of NEXTSTELLIS was presented in a separate integrated review dated April 12, 2021. The current document is for labeling only.
L1441: ## Labeling Review

8 USE IN SPECIFIC POPULATIONS
8.1 Pregnancy
Risk Summary
Discontinue (b) (4) if pregnancy occurs, because there is no reason to use hormonal contraceptives during pregnancy [see Contraindications (4)]. Epidemiologic studies and meta-analyses have not found an increased risk of genital or nongenital birth defects (including cardiac anomalies and limb-reduction defects) following exposure to COCs before conception or during early pregnancy. In the U.S. general population, the estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage in clinically recognized pregnancies is 2 to 4 percent and 15 to 20 percent, respectively.
8 USE IN SPECIFIC POPULATIONS
8.1 Pregnancy
Risk Summary
Discontinue E4/DRSPNEXTSTELLIS if pregnancy occurs, because there is no reason to use hormonal contraceptives during pregnancy [see Contraindications (4)].
Epidemiologic studies and meta-analyses have not found an increased risk of genital or nongenital birth defects (including cardiac anomalies and limb-reduction defects) following exposure to COCs before conception or during early pregnancy. Reproductive toxicity studies performed with E4 alone have shown expected pharmacologic effects in animals, which are considered consistent with estrogen exposure.
In the U.S. general population, the estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage in clinically recognized pregnancies is 2 to 4 percent and 15 to 20 percent, respectively.

L1449: ## Risk Summary
L1450: ## Risk Summary
L1451: Contraceptive hormones and/or metabolites are present in human milk. COCs can reduce milk production in breast-feeding (b) (4). This reduction can occur at any time but is less likely to occur once breast-feeding is well established. When possible, advise the nursing woman to use other methods of contraception until she discontinues breast-feeding [see also *Dosage and Administration* (2.1)]. The developmental and health benefits of breast-feeding should be considered along with the mother's clinical need for (b) (4) and any potential adverse effects on the breast-fed child from (b) (4) or from the underlying maternal condition.

L1462: 12.2 Pharmacodynamics
(b) (4)	12.2 Pharmacodynamics
Drosiprenone is a spironolactone analogue with anti-mineralocorticoid and antiandrogenic activity. The estrogen in NEXTSTELLIS is estetrol, a synthetic analogue of a native estrogen present during pregnancy, that is selective for nuclear estrogen receptor-\alpha (ER-\alpha) and ER-\beta.4

13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY
13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility	13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY
13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility
(b) (4)
In a 24-month oral carcinogenicity study in mice with doses up to 10 mg/kg/day DRSP, equating to 2 times the maximum clinical exposure (based on AUC), there was an increase in carcinomas of the harderian gland in the high dose DRSP group. In a similar study in rats given doses up to 10 mg/kg/day DRSP, 10 times the maximum clinical exposure (based on AUC), there was an increased incidence of benign and total (benign and malignant) adrenal gland pheochromocytomas in the high dose DRSP group. Mutagenesis studies for DRSP were conducted in vivo and in vitro and no evidence of mutagenic activity was observed.
(b) (4)	In a 24-month oral carcinogenicity study in mice with doses up to 10 mg/kg/day DRSP, equating to 2 times the maximum clinical exposure (based on AUC), there was an increase in carcinomas of the harderian gland in the high dose DRSP group. In a similar study in rats given doses up to 10 mg/kg/day DRSP, 10 times the maximum clinical exposure (based on AUC), there was an increased incidence of benign and total (benign and malignant) adrenal gland pheochromocytomas in the high dose DRSP group.
Mutagenesis studies for DRSP were conducted in vivo and in vitro and no evidence of mutagenic activity was observed. E4 is not considered to be genotoxic based on weight of evidence from in vivo and in vitro mutagenesis studies.

L1481: ## APPENDICES
L1482: ## 1 Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1483: Several high performance liquid chromatography tandem mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS) methods were developed and validated for the quantitative determination of E4 and DRSP in human plasma. The lower limits of quantification (LLOQ) were 25.0 pg/mL for E4 and 0.5 ng/mL for DRSP. Detailed methods and validation parameters are described below (Tables 1 to 6 for E4, Tables 7 to 9 for DRSP). The linearity of standard curve, selectivity/specificity, assay accuracy and precision, extraction efficiency and dilution fulfilled the standard acceptance criteria. The established frozen storage stability and run storage stability covered the corresponding study period and sample analysis period.
L1484: The LC-MS/MS method used in Study MIT-Es0001-C110 for the determination of concentrations of E4-C-3 glucuronide and E4-C-16 glucuronide in human plasma samples demonstrated adequate selectivity and sensitivity for both metabolites and showed linearity and acceptable precision and accuracy over the calibration range between 0.250 – 250 ng/mL for E4-C-3 glucuronide and 0.500 – 500 ng/mL for E4-C-16 glucuronide (Validation Report MTA675EL-176753-D). Validation parameters met the established criteria and the method was considered valid for analysis of E4-C-3 glucuronide and E4-C-16 glucuronide in human plasma.

L1505: ## 2 Comparative Bioavailability Studies
L1506: ## 2.1 Study MIT-Es001-CI12 (Relative BA Study)
L1507: **Title:** An open-label, single dose, randomized, two-period, two-treatment, two-way crossover, comparative bioavailability study between a combined oral contraceptive (COC) containing estetrol (E4) 15 mg and drospirenone (DRSP) 3 mg and a COC containing ethinylestradiol (EE) 0.02 mg and DRSP 3 mg (Yaz®) in healthy female volunteers
L1508: ## **Objectives:**
L1509: - To compare the rate and extent of absorption of DRSP after a single oral dose of E4/DRSP 15/3 mg combination (test product) and EE/DRSP 0.02/3 mg combination (reference product) administered under fasting conditions
- To document the general safety of a single oral dose of E4/DRSP 15/3 mg combination and EE/DRSP 0.02/3 mg combination in healthy female volunteers.
L1510: ## **Study Design:**
L1511: This was an open-label, single-center, randomized, two-period, two-treatment, two-way crossover, single-dose bioavailability study in 28 healthy female subjects. All study subjects received both Treatment A and Treatment B either at the first treatment period or the second treatment period. There was a washout period of at least 14 days between treatment periods.
L1512: - Treatment A (Test): Single oral dose of E4/DRSP 15/3 mg tablet
- Treatment B (Reference): Single oral dose of EE/DRSP 0.02/3 mg tablet (Yaz® - US sourced)
L1513: Blood samples for PK analysis were collected at pre-dose, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, 48, 72, 96 and 120 hours post-dose. Determination of DRSP in plasma was performed by [REDACTED] (b) (4) using a validated liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS) method with a linear range of 0.500 – 500 ng/mL (Bioanalytical report MTA19949-19949X-A and method validation report MHUEC302-161697-E).
L1514: ## **PK Results:**
L1515: As shown in **Figure 1**, the mean plasma concentration-time profiles of DRSP following oral administration of E4/DRSP and EE/DRSP (Yaz®) are superimposed (PK parameters in **Table 2**). With Yaz® as the reference product and E4/DRSP as the test product, the calculated 90% confidence interval (CI) for ratio of geometric means (GMR) of C_{max} and AUC_{0-\infty} fell well within the 80% to 125% range (**Table 3**), indicating bioequivalence on DRSP between E4/DRSP and Yaz®.

AUC0-inf (ng*hr/mL)	26	504	26	550	91.65	87.89, 95.56
L1521: ## **Reviewer's Comments:**
L1522: *The applicant proposed to rely on the Agency's safety findings of DRSP from the listed drug Yaz®, an approved COC containing the same dose of DRSP as to E4/DRSP and thus conducted this PK bridging study. It should be noted that the current study only demonstrated that DRSP exposure from E4/DRSP is comparable to that from Yaz® under the single dose. Nonetheless, considering the accumulation ratios of DRSP upon multiple dosing are about 2.3 and 2.8 (based on AUC values) for E4/DRSP 15/3 mg and Yaz®, respectively, it is expected that steady state DRSP exposure from E4/DRSP will be similar or lower than that from Yaz®.*
L1523: ## **2.2 Study MIT-Es001-C101 (Food Effect Study)**
L1524: **Title:** A study to characterize the effect of food on the bioavailability of 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP tablets in healthy female volunteers
L1525: ## **Objectives:**
L1526: - To assess the effect of a high fat meal on the bioavailability of E4 and DRSP following oral administration of an E4/DRSP 15/3 mg tablet.
- To assess and compare the general safety and tolerability of single dose administrations of E4/DRSP 15/3 mg when given alone in fasted and fed conditions.
L1527: ## **Study Design:**
L1528: The study was a single-center, open-label, randomized, balanced, single-dose, two-treatment, two-period, two-sequence crossover study conducted in 24 healthy female volunteers. All subjects received both Treatment A and Treatment B at the first treatment period or the second treatment period with a washout period of 28 days:
L1529: - Treatment A (Reference): A single E4/DRSP 15/3 mg tablet administered orally following an overnight fast of at least 10 hours (fasted).
- Treatment B (Test): A single E4/DRSP 15/3 mg tablet administered orally 30 min after the start of an FDA prescribed high-fat breakfast (1000 calories, 50% fat) preceded by at least a 10-hour overnight fast (fed).
L1530: Blood samples were collected prior to each dose and at 10, 20, 30 and 45 min, and then at 1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, 48 and 72 hours post-dose for PK analysis of E4 and DRSP. Bioanalytical samples were analyzed for E4 and DRSP plasma concentrations using validated Ultrahigh pressure liquid chromatography tandem mass spectrometry (UHPLC-MS/MS) methods. The bioanalytical reports are AR009 for E4 and AR010 for DRSP. The method validation reports are VR006 for E4 and VR007 for DRSP.
L1531: ## **Study Results:**
L1532: The rate (C_{max}) of E4 and DRSP absorption following oral administration of a single E4/DRSP 15/3 mg tablet in fed conditions was slightly lower than in fasted conditions. This observation was more pronounced for the rate of E4 absorption than for the rate of DRSP absorption. The extent (AUC) of E4 and DRSP absorption following oral administration of a single E4/DRSP 15/3 mg tablet in fed conditions was equivalent to the one in fasted conditions. Administration of a single 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP tablet was considered safe and well tolerated. In conclusion, high fat meal decreased the rate, but not the extent of E4 and DRSP absorption following oral administration.

L1538: ## 3 Human Pharmacokinetic Studies
L1539: ## 3.1 Study MIT-Es0001-C105 (Mass Balance Study)
L1540: **Title:** An open-label, single dose study designed to assess the mass balance recovery, metabolite profiles and metabolite identification of [^{14}C] E4 in healthy female volunteers
L1541: ## Objectives:
L1542: - To characterize the absorption, metabolism and excretion of E4 in healthy female volunteers.
- To characterize safety and tolerability of a single dose of E4.
L1543: ## Study Design:
L1544: This study was a Phase 1, open-label, non-randomized, single dose study in 6 healthy female subjects of non-child bearing potential. On Day 1 of the study, each subject received a single oral dose of carbon-14 [^{14}C]-E4 15 mg administered as a solution containing approximately 76 \mu Ci ^{14}C. Blood, urine and fecal samples were taken at specified time points throughout the study for the analysis of total radioactivity in whole blood, plasma, urine and faeces, and estetrol and metabolite profiling in plasma, urine and feces. Bioanalytical samples were analyzed for E4 plasma and urine concentrations using validated LC-MS/MS methods. The bioanalytical reports are (b) (4) 277511QB05 (plasma) and (b) (4) 277511QB05 (urine) and method validation reports are (b) (4) 277511QB03 (plasma) and (b) (4) 277511QB04 (urine).
L1545: ## Study Results:
L1546: As shown in Figure 1, following a single oral solution dose of [^{14}C]-E4 15 mg, 90.85% of the total administered dose of radioactivity was recovered in the sampling period of 312 hours post-dose with 68.97% in the urine and 21.88% in feces.
L1547: **Figure 1 Arithmetic mean mass balance recoveries of total radioactivity in plasma, urine and feces following a single oral dose of 15 mg [^{14}C]-E4**
L1548: ## Plasma PK of Total Radioactivity following Oral Administration of 15 mg [^{14}C]-E4
L1549: Following a single oral solution dose of 15 mg [^{14}C]-E4, maximum concentrations for total radioactivity were reached between 0.25 h and 0.5 h post-dose; thereafter, plasma concentrations of total radioactivity declined in a biphasic manner (Figure 2, Table 1). The appearance of secondary peaks in PK plots for all matrices suggest the possibility of enterohepatic recycling. The AUC_{(0-last)} of the [^{14}C]-E4 accounts for only 15.8% of the AUC_{(0-last)} for the total radioactivity, suggesting that 84.2% of the radioactive dose is circulating as metabolites. The geometric mean whole blood to plasma total radioactivity ratios ranged from 0.352 to 0.705 with no apparent trend in data over the time course, indicating limited distribution of [^{14}C]-E4 into red blood cells.
L1550: **Figure 2** Geometric mean (SD) [^{14}C]-E4 and total radioactivity concentrations in plasma following a single oral dose of 15 mg [^{14}C]-E4 (Study MIT-Es0001-C105)
L1551: The observed geometric mean (CV%) renal clearance for E4 was fairly low at only 8.10 (41.6) mL/min. However, the renal clearance for total radioactivity was much higher at 288 (27.6) mL/min, suggesting an overall net secretion of E4 metabolites when compared to the glomerular filtration rate of 125 mL/min.

L1557: ## Metabolite Profiling
L1558: Direct conjugation of E4 with glucuronic acid (E4 glucuronide) and with both glucuronide and sulfate (glucuronide &amp; sulfate conjugate of E4) are the major circulating metabolites in plasma. Two glucuronide metabolites consist &gt;90% of radioactivity in urine. Only E4, but no metabolites, was found in feces. A biotransformation pathway for the metabolism of Estetrol in human is shown in Figure 2.
L1559: **Figure 2 Proposed Biotransformation Pathway**
L1560: Diagram illustrating the proposed biotransformation pathway of Estetrol (P5, F1).

The pathway shows Estetrol (P5, F1) converting to two major metabolites:

- Glucuronide &amp; Sulfate Conjugate of Estetrol (P2)
- Estetrol Glucuronide (isomer 1: P1, U1), (isomer 2: P4, U4)
The diagram shows the chemical structures of Estetrol, the glucuronide conjugate, and the glucuronide &amp; sulfate conjugate.
L1561: ## Reviewer's Comments:
L1562: - Although there is no dedicated study to determine the absolute bioavailability of E4, based on the recovery of 69% of total administered radioactivity in urine, absolute bioavailability of E4 is estimated to be \ge 69%.
- There was 21.88% of administered radioactivity recovered in feces, which was only consisted of E4. Since E4 is a highly permeable compound, unabsorbed E4 is not likely to account for the whole E4 amount found in feces. Therefore, this finding may suggest a possible biliary excretion. In vitro studies show that E4 is a substrate of P-gp and BCRP. Considering the relatively small contribution of potential biliary excretion to the overall clearance of E4, clinical DDI studies with strong P-gp or BCRP inhibitors are not warranted.
- The appearance of secondary peaks in the time vs concentration plots for all matrices may indicate enterohepatic recirculation, i.e., intestinal reabsorption following biliary excretion. It should be also noted that the secondary peaks are small relative to the main peak.
L1563: ## 3.2 Study MIT-Es0001-C103 (Single and Multiple Ascending Dose)
L1564: **Title:** A randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel, single center study to investigate the
L1565: pharmacokinetics, safety, tolerability, and QT concentration-effect modelling of estetrol (E4) in combination with drospirenone (DRSP) after single and multiple dosing in healthy women
L1566: ## **Objectives:**
L1567: - Primary: To investigate the PK of single and multiple doses of E4 in combination with DRSP in healthy women.
- Secondary: To investigate the safety and tolerability of single and multiple doses of E4 in combination with DRSP in healthy women and to define the effect of E4 in combination with DRSP on QT interval in healthy women using exposure-response analysis.
L1568: ## **Study Design:**
L1569: This was a Phase 1, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel, single and multiple oral dose study in 55 healthy adult subjects with BMI ranging from 18.5 to 34.1 kg/m2. During the study, subjects were randomized to one of the four treatment groups. Within each dose group, 10 subjects received E4/DRSP treatment and 4 subjects received placebo (**Table 1**). Subjects received a single dose on Day 1 and after a 14 days washout period, they received multiple doses for E4/DRSP for 14 consecutive days from Days 15 to 28.
L1570: Blood samples to determine plasma concentrations of DRSP and E4 on Day 1 and Day 28 were collected at pre-dose, at 15 and 30 minutes post-dose and 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, 48, 72, 96, 120, 144, and 168 h post-dose. Blood samples were also collected at pre-dose on Days 17, 19, 21, 23, 25, and 27. Plasma concentrations of DRSP and E4 were analyzed using validated LC/MS/MS assays with linear analytical range of 500 to 100000 pg/mL, and 25 to 25000 pg/mL respectively. The bioanalytical reports and validation reports are MTA738EC-157813-C and MTA738EC-157813-B for E4 and MTA738EC-157813-F and MTA738EC-157813-E for DRSP.
L1571: **Table 1 Treatments groups for Study MIT-Es0001-C103**
L1572: N
E4/DRSP / placebo	Dose level
E4 (mg)	Dose level
DRSP (mg)	Dosage
Group 1	10 / 4	15	3	therapeutic E4/DRSP dose level
Group 2	10 / 4	30	6	2 times therapeutic E4/DRSP dose level
Group 3	10 / 4	60	12	4 times therapeutic E4/DRSP dose level (supratherapeutic dose)
Group 4	9 / 4	75	15	5 times therapeutic E4/DRSP dose level (supratherapeutic dose)
L1573: ## **Study Results**
L1574: Plasma concentration-time profiles of E4 and DRSP after single- and multiple- ascending doses of E4/DRSP are presented in **Figure 1** and **Figure 2** with PK parameters summarized in **Table 2** and **Table 3**.
L1575: - Mean Cmax and AUC0-t values of E4 and DRSP increased in a dose proportional manner with escalating doses of E4/DRSP from 15/3 mg to 75/15 mg.
- Median Tmax values ranged from 0.5 to 1.26 h for E4 and 1 to 2 h for DRSP following single- and multiple-dose administration of E4/DRSP.
L1576: - Geometric mean t_{1/2} values were similar following single-dose and multiple-dose administration of E4/DRSP and ranged from 20.2 to 24.3 h for E4 and from 28.4 to 37.1 h for DRSP across 5 treatment groups.
- Mean accumulation ratio ranged from 1.62 to 1.87 for E4 and from 2.32 to 2.58 for DRSP.
- Following multiple dose administrations, steady-state E4 concentrations appeared to have been reached after 4 days of dosing for the 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP and 30 mg E4/6 mg DRSP treatments, after 6 days of dosing for the 60 mg E4/12 mg DRSP treatment, and after 12 days of dosing for the 75 mg E4/15 mg DRSP treatment.
- Following multiple dose administrations, steady-state DRSP concentrations appeared to have been reached after 10 days of dosing for all E4/DRSP treatments.
- Single and 14-day multiple oral dose administrations of E4/DRSP in the range of E4/DRSP 15/3 to 75/15 mg were safe and well-tolerated by healthy female subjects.
- No serious AE were reported in this study. There was no obvious increase in either percentage of subjects reporting TEAEs or the number of TEAEs with increasing single and multiple E4/DRSP doses. There were no findings of clinical relevance with respect to clinical laboratory, vital signs, 12-lead ECG, echocardiography or physical examination.

L1583: ## 3.3 Study MIT-Es0001-C102 (E4 Single and Multiple Ascending Dose in Women)
L1584: **Title:** An open-label, single center, randomized, two period study to characterize the safety, tolerability and PK of E4 after single and multiple oral doses in healthy female volunteers
L1585: ## **Objectives:**
L1586: - To determine the PK profile of E4 after single oral dose of E4 5, 15 or 45 mg and the PK profile of E4 after multiple oral doses of E4 15 mg during a 14-day period.
- To assess the general safety and tolerability of single oral dose of E4 5, 15 or 45 mg and multiple oral doses of E4 15 mg for 14 days.
L1587: ## **Study Design:**
L1588: This was a Phase 1, single center, open-label, randomized, two-period, single and multiple oral dose study in 27 healthy adult female subjects. During Period 1 (single dose), study subjects were randomized to one of the three dose groups (5 mg, 15 mg and 45 mg). After at least a 14 days washout period, 18 subjects were scheduled to take E4 15 mg once daily for 14 consecutive days (Period 2, multiple dose). Blood samples were collected on Period 1 Day 1 and on Period 2 Day 14 at the following time points: at pre-dose and at 10, 20, 30, 45 minutes and then at 1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, 48, 72, 96, 120, 144 and 168 hours post-dose. In Period 2, additional blood samples were collected at pre-dose on Day 1 and every 2 days between Day 2 and Day 12. Plasma concentrations of E4 were analyzed using a validated LC/MS/MS assay. The bioanalytical report and validation report are AR3010 and PVR4011.
L1589: ## **Study Results**
L1590: As shown in **Figure 1**, PK profiles of E4 after single dose administration of 5, 15 and 45 mg E4 are characterized by an initial quick absorption phase with T_{max} observed between 0.33 hour and 1.5 hours, a decrease generally followed by a secondary absorption peak up to 24 hours, and then the terminal mono-exponential elimination phase from 24 hours onwards. Following multiple oral dose administration for 14 days of one E4 15 mg tablet daily, the PK steady-state was achieved within 6 to 8 days as estimated from the E4 trough concentrations. Comparison of PK parameters achieved after 15 mg multiple dose administration and after 15 mg single dose indicates there was no increase of the peak E4 systemic concentration (C_{max}) and a less than 2-fold increase in geometric mean (GM) E4 exposure over one dosing interval (AUC_{0-\tau}), as indicated by a GM accumulation ratio (R_{AC}) of 1.80 (**Table 1**). E4 PK is dose proportional in the dose range of 5 mg - 45 mg as measured by C_{max} and AUC values. Overall, no remarkable safety findings were observed in this study.

L1595: ## 3.4 Study PR3050 (E4 Single Ascending Dose in Women)
L1596: **Title:** A double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, single rising dose study to evaluate the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of estetrol in healthy postmenopausal volunteers
L1597: ## **Objective:**
L1598: To investigate the safety, tolerability and PK of E4 following single administration of escalating doses: E4 0.1, 1, 10 and 100 mg
L1599: ## **Study Design:**
L1600: This was a Phase 1, double-blind, single dose, randomized, placebo-controlled, first-in-man study in 32 healthy adult female subjects. During the study, subjects were randomized to one of the four dose groups (0.1, 1, 10 and 100 mg). In each dosing cohort, 6 subjects received E4 treatment and 2 subjects received placebo treatment. For safety reasons, subjects in the 100 mg dose group were treated with 10 mg dydrogesterone for 10 days starting 14 days after dosing to counteract any stimulatory effect on the endometrium. Blood samples were collected at 0 (just prior to dosing), 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00, 1.30, 2.00, 3.00, 4.00, 6.00, 8.00, 12.00, 16.00, 24.00, 30.00, 36.00, 48.00, and 72.00 hours after dosing (total of 18 PK samples per subject). Plasma concentrations of E4 were analyzed using a validated LC/MS/MS assay with validation reports of PR3053-VR0719, -VR0671, -VP200.
L1601: ## **Study Results**
L1602: Plasma concentration profiles could not be determined in subjects of the 0.1 mg group. The plasma concentration profiles of E4 following oral administration of single doses of 1, 10 and 100 mg are shown in Figure 1 with PK parameters summarized in Table 1. The absorption of E4 was fast with Tmax around 0.25 - 0.5 hours. Reabsorption was evident during the first 18 h after oral intake. E4 was eliminated with a terminal half-life of 28 hours (ranges 18 - 60 h). AUC0-∞ values were proportional to the oral dose in the range from 10 to 100 mg E4. Cmax values were proportional in the range from 1 to 100 mg E4. The terminal half-life and fractional clearance values were not affected by dose level. Overall, no remarkable safety findings were observed in this study.
L1603: Figure 1 Arithmetic mean plasma concentrations of E4 following a single dose oral administration of 1 mg, 10 mg and 100 mg E4 (Study PR3050, N= 6/group)

L1606: ## 3.5 Study PR3054 (E4 Multiple Ascending Dose in Women)
L1607: **Title:** A phase I, partly randomized, open-label, multiple dose study to evaluate the safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of 3 dosages of E4, the lowest dose of 2 mg estetrol compared with 2 mg of E4, after daily oral administration for 28 days in healthy postmenopausal women
L1608: ## **Objective:**
L1609: To investigate the safety, tolerability and PK of E4 following multiple administration of escalating doses: E4 10, 20 and 40 mg
L1610: ## **Study Design:**
L1611: This was a Phase 1, partly randomized, open label study in 49 healthy adult female subjects. During the study, subjects were randomized to one of the five treatment groups, i.e., 2 mg E4, 2 mg E2-valerate, 10 mg, 20 mg and 40 mg E4. Subjects were dosed with E2-valerate or E4 once daily for 28 days. For groups 1-3, blood samples were collected on Days 1, 2, 7, 14, and 28 just prior to dosing and on Days 29, 30 and 31, at approximately the clock time of Day 1 to Day 28 dosing. Blood samples were also collected on Days 1 and 28 at 15, 30 and 60 min after intake of the study medication. For Groups 4 and 5, though
L1612: samples were collected on Days 2, 7, 14, and 28 just prior to dosing. In addition, blood samples were collected on Day 28 at 10, 20, 30, and 45 min and at 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, and 72 h after dosing. Plasma concentrations of E4 were analyzed using a validated LC/MS/MS assay with validation reports of PR3053-VR0719, -VR0671, -VP200.
L1613: ## Study Results
L1614: The absorption of E4 is fast (T_{max}: 0.2 – 0.5h) both on Day 1 and on Day 28. Steady-state mean plasma concentrations were reached largely during the second week of dosing. The terminal elimination half-life could not accurately be determined because of the limited sampling schedule. Following repeated doses, over the entire dose range (2 to 40 mg), the 20-fold increase in the dose resulted in an over-proportional increase of approximately 55-fold of geometric mean C_{max} (from 1.27 to 72.3 ng/mL). The AUC_{0-24} data for the 20 and 40 mg groups revealed that the increase in the 40 mg group was approximately proportional to the dose increase. The inter-subject variability for the PK parameters C_{max} and AUC ranged between 19% and 62%. E4 up to a dose of 40 mg/day given for 28 days is safe and well tolerated. No dose-dependent AE's were noted, except for the incidence of nipple tenderness that seemed to increase in an E4 dose-dependent manner.
L1615: **Figure 1 Arithmetic mean plasma concentrations of E4 from 6 to 72 h post-dose on Day 28 for all dose groups (Study PR3054)**

L1619: ## 4 Human Pharmacodynamic Studies
L1620: ## 4.1 Study ES-C01/PR3095 (Dose-finding Study on ovulation inhibition)
L1621: **Title:** A dose-finding study with active control group Yaz® to assess the contraceptive efficacy and the effect on liver function of 5 or 10 mg E4 combined with either 3 mg DRSP or 150 µg levonorgestrel, or 20 mg E4 combined with 150 µg levonorgestrel, by daily oral administration to healthy female volunteers for 3 cycles of 24 days each followed by a 4-day treatment pause
L1622: **Objectives:** To evaluate the effect of various dose of E4/DRSP on ovulation inhibition and liver function and PK of E4
L1623: ## **Study Design:**
L1624: This was an open label, parallel dose-finding study in 110 healthy pre-menopausal women. During the study, the subjects were randomized to one of the six treatment groups below:
L1625: - Group 1: 5 mg E4 combined with 3 mg DRSP
- Group 2: 10 mg E4 combined with 3 mg DRSP
- Group 3: 20 µg EE combined with 3 mg DRSP (Yaz®)
- Group 4: 5 mg E4 combined with 150 µg LNG
- Group 5: 10 mg E4 combined with 150 µg LNG
- Group 6: 20 mg E4 combined with 150 µg LNG
L1626: Subjects in all treatment groups received the study medication for a period of 3 consecutive treatment cycles (24 days active treatment followed by a 4-day treatment pause or placebo treatment). Steady-state E4 trough concentrations were determined in the E4 treatment groups on Day 24 of treatment Cycle 1 and Cycle 3. The excretion of E4 and E4-conjugates in urine was investigated by collecting 24-hour urine at steady-state from 10 subjects in the E4 10 mg treatment groups. Ovulation inhibition was assessed by rating the suppression of the ovaries using the Hoogland Score, which is based on the follicle sizes assessed by Trans-vaginal ultrasound (TVUS) and the estradiol (E2) and progesterone (P) levels in Cycle 1 and Cycle 3 (see protocol for detailed sampling schemes).
L1627: ## **PK Results:**
L1628: As shown in **Table 1**, plasma E4 trough levels followed a dose-dependent trend with the highest mean levels reported with the E4 20 mg dose level. For the E4 5 mg and 10 mg treatment groups, the mean plasma E4 trough levels were slightly lower in the treatment group who received LNG compared with those who received E4 with DRSP. E4 was primarily excreted in the urine as E4-glucuronide and only a small amount of E4-sulfate. Urinary excretion of unconjugated E4 was low.

L1631: ## **PD Results:**
L1632: Treatment with 5 or 10 mg E4 combined with either 3 mg DRSP or 0.15 mg LNG, or E4 20 mg combined with 0.15 mg LNG inhibited ovulation in all investigated cycles. In the 3 LNG treatment groups, there is a dose-dependent effect of E4 on ovulation suppression with maximal effect in the E4 20 mg plus LNG treatment group based on Hoogland scores (Table 2).

L1636: ## **Reviewer's Comments:**
L1637: *In the 3 DRSP treatment group, although ovulation inhibition has been achieved in all groups, it appears 0.02 mg EE had a relatively stronger synergistic effect on suppressing the ovarian activity compared to 5 or 10 mg E4. We defer to clinical reviewer on the assessment on liver function.*
L1638: ## **4.2 Study ES-C02 (Dose-finding Study on bleeding control)**
L1639: **Title:** A randomized, open-label, multicenter, dose-finding study to evaluate cycle control of 15 mg or 20 mg estetrol combined with either 150 µg levonorgestrel or 3 mg drospirenone, compared to a combined oral contraceptive containing estradiol valerate and dienogest
L1640: **Primary Objective:** To assess vaginal bleeding patterns (cycle control) of E4 15 mg and 20 mg combined with either LNG 0.15 mg or DRSP 3 mg during a 24:4 treatment regimen.
L1641: ## **Study Design:**
L1642: This was a Phase 2, randomized, open label, parallel dose-finding study in 389 healthy pre-menopausal women. During the study, the subjects were randomized to one of the five treatment groups below and received treatment for six treatment cycles (Table 1). Cycle control was evaluated on the basis of vaginal bleeding patterns and tablet intake as recorded daily by subjects using study diaries. Unscheduled bleeding is defined as any bleeding or spotting reported during the expected non-bleeding interval, i.e.,
L1643: during Cycle Days 5–24. Scheduled bleeding is defined as vaginal bleeding or spotting reported to occur during the expected bleeding interval (Days 25 through 4).
L1644: **Table 1 Treatment groups in Study ES-C02**
L1645: Group	Treatment	Treatment schedule/cycle	Number of subjects
Group 1	Qlaira® (E2V 1/2/3 mg plus DNG 0/2/3 mg)	Days 1-26: 1 tablet Qlaira/day
Days 27-28: 1 placebo tablet/day	N = 78
Group 2	E4/LNG 20/0.15 mg	Days 1-24: 1 tablet E4 + 1 tablet LNG/day
Days 25-28: 2 placebo tablets/day	N = 77
Group 3	E4/DRSP 20/3 mg	Days 1-24: 1 tablet E4 + 1 tablet DRSP/day
Days 25-28: 2 placebo tablets/day	N = 75
Group 4	E4/LNG 15/0.15 mg	Days 1-24: 1 tablet E4 + 1 tablet LNG/day
Days 25-28: 2 placebo tablets/day	N = 80
Group 5	E4/DRSP 15/3 mg	Days 1-24: 1 tablet E4 + 1 tablet DRSP/day
Days 25-28: 2 placebo tablets/day	N = 79
L1646: ## Study Results
L1647: ## Unscheduled bleeding/spotting
L1648: Unscheduled bleeding-spotting was reported by &gt;40% of subjects in any cycle (Figure 1). By Cycle 6, the E4/DRSP 15/3 mg group had the lowest incidence of all groups (33.8% compared to 47.8% in the E2V/DNG group) resulting in a difference of 13.9% fewer subjects with unscheduled bleeding/spotting than in the E2V/DNG group. The incidence of unscheduled bleeding/spotting was 41.5% in the E4 20 mg/LNG group and 48.3% in the E4 15 mg/LNG group in treatment Cycle 6.
L1649: Unscheduled bleeding was the highest in the E2V/DNG and E4 15 mg/LNG (Qlaira groups, 35.8-45.5%), and the lowest in the E4/DRSP groups (16.9-30.6%). In Cycle 6, unscheduled bleeding was reported by &lt;20% of subjects in the E4 20 mg/LNG group (18.9% of subjects) and in the E4 15 mg/DRSP group (16.9% of subjects). In all treatment groups in all other treatment cycles unscheduled bleeding was reported by &gt;20% of subjects (except Cycle 4 for E4 20 mg/DRSP).
L1650: **Figure 1 Percentage of subjects with unscheduled bleeding/spotting by cycle (Study ES-C02, N=378)**
L1651: ## Absence of scheduled (withdrawal) bleeding:
L1652: As shown in **Figure 2**, absence of scheduled bleeding occurred in &lt;20% of subjects in all E4 treatment groups throughout the study and in a much lower proportion than in the E2V/DNG group (27.1% of subjects in treatment Cycle 6). The percentage of subjects that did not have scheduled bleeding was very low in the E4/DRSP groups in the primary analysis cycles (Cycles 2, 3 and 6): no more than two subjects (&lt;4%) had an absence of scheduled bleeding in any treatment cycle throughout the study (Figure 2). In contrast, between 14.0% and 18.5% of subjects in the E4/LNG groups and 27.1% of subjects in the E2V/DNG group reported the absence of withdrawal bleeding in Cycle 6.
L1653: **Figure 2 Percentage of subjects without scheduled bleeding by cycle (Study ES-C02; N=378)**
L1654: Overall, the E4/DRSP 15/3 mg group showed the lowest incidence of unscheduled intra-cyclic bleeding in Cycle 6 (16.9% of subjects), as well as the lowest incidence of absence of scheduled bleeding (3.5% at treatment Cycle 6), and the lowest number of bleeding days and bleeding-spotting days within bleeding episodes, and an acceptable safety profile.
L1655: ## 4.3 Study MIT-Es0001-C202 (Dose-confirmation Study)
L1656: **Title:** A single-center, randomized, open-label, two-arm study to evaluate the ovarian function inhibition of a monophasic combined contraceptive (COC) containing 15 mg estetrol (E4) and 3 mg drosiprenone (DRSP) and a monophasic COC containing 20 mcg ethinylestradiol (EE)/3 mg DRSP (YAZ®), administered orally once daily in a 24/4 day regimen for three consecutive cycles
L1657: **Primary Objective:** To evaluate the effects of the E4/DRSP 15/3 mg combination and the EE/DRSP 0.02/3 mg combination (Yaz®) used as reference on ovarian function inhibition at treatment Cycle 1 and treatment Cycle 3.
L1658: ## Study Design:
L1659: This was a Phase 2, single-center, randomized, open-label, controlled, 2-arm study in 82 healthy premenopausal women. During the study, the subjects were randomized to one of two treatment groups (E4/DRSP 15/3 mg or Yaz®) and received treatment for three treatment cycles. Ovulation inhibition was assessed using the Hoogland Score, which is based on the follicle sizes assessed by Trans-vaginal ultrasound (TVUS) and the estradiol (E2) and progesterone (P) levels in Cycle 1 and Cycle 3 (see protocol for detailed sampling schemes). Levels of LH, FSH, E2, and progesterone in serum on cycle day 3, 6, 9, 12, 15, 18, 21, 24, 27 (all days \pm1 day) at treatment Cycle 1 and treatment Cycle 3 and on cycle day 3 (\pm1 day) of treatment Cycle 2. Double layer endometrial thickness measured by TVUS on all visit days of all treatment cycles. Return to fertility measured by monitoring follicular growth using TVUS every three days from cycle day 3 of the post-treatment cycle until ovulation occurred or until cycle day 36 (all days \pm1 day) and confirmed by serum progesterone.
L1660: ## Study Results
L1661: No ovulations were observed in the E4/DRSP group, while in the EE/DRSP treatment group, three ovulations were observed in two subjects throughout the treatment period. Hoogland scores, follicle diameter, and E2, progesterone, FSH, and LH levels were all reflective of strong ovarian suppression in both treatment groups. Hoogland scores were similar between both treatment groups in Cycle 1, but slightly higher with E4/DRSP than with EE/DRSP in Cycle 3, due to slightly larger follicle diameters (**Figure 1**). E2 levels were slightly lower for E4/DRSP throughout treatment while progesterone levels were similarly low in both treatment groups, except for some peaks seen in the EE/DRSP treatment group due to the ovulations observed in this group. FSH and LH levels were slightly higher with E4/DRSP than with EE/DRSP (**Figure 2**). During treatment cycles, mean endometrial thickness was constant for both treatments, and below 5 mm. Resumption of ovulation was demonstrated for both treatments and was slightly faster after treatment with E4/DRSP (15.5 days) than after treatment with EE/DRSP (18.1 days). Both E4/DRSP and EE/DRSP treatments were generally safe and well tolerated.
L1662: **Figure 1 Hoogland Scores Results (Study MIT-Es0001-C202)**
L1663: FLS = follicle-like structure, LUF = luteinized unruptured follicle, N = number of subjects
L1664: **Figure 2 Mean hormone concentrations over time (Study MIT-Es0001-C202)**
L1665: ## 5 Intrinsic and Extrinsic Factor PK Studies
L1666: ## 5.1 Study MIT-Es0001-C109 (Effect of Race)
L1667: **Title:** A single-center, double-blind, placebo-controlled, randomized study to compare the pharmacokinetic profile of different dosages of estetrol (E4) combined with drospirenone (DRSP) or E4 alone between Japanese and Caucasian populations
L1668: ## Objectives:
L1669: - To characterize and compare the PK profiles of E4 and DRSP in Japanese and Caucasian subjects.
- To assess the safety and tolerability of a single-dose administration of the combination of E4 and DRSP, and of E4 alone, in Japanese and Caucasian subjects.
L1670: ## Study Design
L1671: This was a Phase 1, single-center, double-blind, placebo-controlled, randomized study in 80 healthy premenopausal Japanese and Caucasian women (40/ethnic group). Subjects received single doses of E4/DRSP, E4 15 mg tablets or placebo (Table 1). Blood samples for the analysis of E4 and DRSP in plasma were collected within 60 minutes prior to the study drug administration and postdose at 10, 20, 30 and 45 minutes, and at 1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14, 16, 24, 30, 36, 48 and 72 hours (24 samples in total). Plasma concentrations of DRSP and E4 were analyzed using validated LC/MS/MS
L1672: assays. The bioanalytical reports and validation reports are MHUEC302-161697-C and MHUEC302-161697-B for E4 and MHUEC302-161697-F and MHUEC302-161697-E for DRSP.
L1673: **Table 1 Subject disposition in Study MIT-Es0001-C109**
L1674: Group	1	2	3	4	Total number of subjects
E4/DRSP dose	E4/DRSP 5/3 mg	E4/DRSP 15/3 mg	E4/DRSP 20/3 mg	E4 15 mg	
N by treatment group	
Japanese	10 / 2	10 / 2	10 / 2	10 / 2	40 / 8
Caucasian	10 / 2	10 / 2	10 / 2	10 / 2	40 / 8
Total	80 / 16
L1675: "N by treatment group" represents number of subjects in active treatment/placebo groups.
L1676: ## **Study Results (Table 1 and Table 2)**
L1677: - In the presence of DRSP (3 mg), E4 C_{max}, AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf} increased in a dose proportional manner for Japanese and Caucasian subjects across the dose range studied (5, 15 and 20 mg).
- At 15 mg, E4 displayed similar exposure (in terms of AUC) for Japanese and Caucasian subjects with or without the presence of DRSP (3 mg).
- The geometric means ratios of "Japanese/Caucasian" for PK parameters C_{max}, AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf} were 0.99, 1.04 and 1.09, respectively.
- In the presence of E4 (5, 15 and 20 mg), DRSP C_{max}, AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf} remained constant for Japanese and Caucasian subjects.
- The geometric means ratios of "Japanese/Caucasian" for the PK parameters C_{max}, AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf} were 1.04, 1.04 and 1.07, respectively.
- No significant differences in safety and tolerability observed between Japanese and Caucasian subjects in the study. All TEAEs were mild in severity.
L1678: Overall, there are no relevant PK differences between Japanese and Caucasian subjects.

L1684: ## Reviewer's Comments:
L1685: *It appears that there is no drug-drug interaction between E4 and DRSP based on the following:*
L1686: - *At 15 mg, E4 AUC values are comparable with or without the presence of 3 mg DRSP.*
- *DRSP AUC values remain constant in the presence of 5, 15 and 20 mg E4.*
L1687: ## 5.2 Study MIT-Es0001-C110 (DDI Study)
L1688: **Title:** An open-label, two-center, two-way cross-over study to determine the effect of multiple doses of valproic acid (VAL) on the pharmacokinetics and safety of a single oral dose of estetrol/drospirenone in healthy female subjects
L1689: ## **Objectives:**
L1690: - To evaluate the effect of multiple oral doses of 500 mg valproic acid administered twice daily (bid) on the PK of E4, DRSP and E4-glucuronide metabolites.
- To evaluate the safety of a single oral dose of E4/DRSP, alone or in the presence of VAL
L1691: ## **Study Design:**
L1692: This was a Phase 1, open-label, two-center, two-way crossover trial in 24 healthy adult female subjects. During the study, subjects received two treatments as either treatment Sequence A-B or B-A with 14 to 21 days washout period between treatments (**Table 1**):
L1693: - Treatment A: Single oral dose of E4/DRSP 15/3 mg
L1694: - Treatment B: Multiple oral doses of 500 mg VAL tablet bid for 11 days (with a 12-hour interval between doses), with a final single dose on Day 12. On the 5th day of VAL administration, subjects received one single oral dose of E4/DRSP 15/3 mg, 2 hours after the morning VAL administration.
L1695: Blood samples for PK (E4, DRSP and E4-glucuronide metabolites) were taken pre-dose (\le 10 min before dosing) and 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.25, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, 48, 72, 96, 120, 144, and 168 hours starting post-dose on Day 1 of Period 1 and Day 5 of Period 2. Determination of E4, DRSP, and E4-glucuronide metabolites in human plasma was performed by validated liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry methods. The lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) was 25 pg/mL for E4, 0.5 ng/mL for DRSP and C-16 glucuronide, and 0.25 ng/mL for C-3 glucuronide.

L1700: ## **Study Results:**
L1701: The mean plasma concentration vs time profiles of E4 up to 12 hours post-dose are shown in Figure 1. The results showed that co-administration of UGT2B7 inhibitor VAL with E4/DRSP increased AUC_{0-16h}, AUC_{0-\infty} and C_{max} of E4 by 1.24-, 1.13- and 1.36-fold, respectively (Table 2). The metabolite to parent ratios decreased from 2.45 to 2.0 for E4-3-glucuronide and from 4.8 to 2.3 for E4-16-glucuronide in the presence of valproic acid (Table 3 and Table 4). There was no impact on DRSP PK (Table 5). There were no SAEs or severe TEAEs. Moderate TEAEs were reported by 5 subjects; the majority of TEAEs were mild. No safety parameter measurements were considered clinically significant and reported as AEs, with the exception of one subject withdrawn owing to a decreased neutrophil count. Overall, although the ratios of E4 AUC and C_{max} with and without VAL coadministration fall outside the bioequivalence limits of 80% to 125%, the magnitude of the E4 increase was small and not considered as clinically relevant.

L1715: ## 6. Mechanistic Safety Assessment
L1716: ## 6.1 Study MIT-Es0001-C106 (QTc Study)
L1717: **Title:** A phase 1, randomized, double-blind, multiple-dose, parallel group with a nested crossover design study to investigate the effects of therapeutic and supratherapeutic concentrations of estetrol (E4) in combination with drosipenone (DRSP) on the heart rate corrected QT interval (QTc) in healthy women
L1718: ## Objectives:
L1719: - To evaluate the effects of therapeutic and supratherapeutic concentrations of estetrol (E4) in combination with drosipenone (DRSP) on the heart rate (HR)-corrected QT interval (QTc) on Day 10 after dosing with 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP and on Day 20 following 10 days of dosing with up to a maximum of 75 mg E4/15 mg DRSP
- To assess the pharmacokinetics (PK) of E4/DRSP, following administration of therapeutic and supratherapeutic multiple oral doses in healthy subjects.
L1720: ## Study Design:
L1721: This was a placebo- and active-controlled multiple-dose, parallel group study in 64 healthy adult female subjects to evaluate the effects of therapeutic and supratherapeutic concentrations of E4 in combination with DRSP on the heart rate (HR) corrected QT interval (QTc). Subjects in Group 1 (N = 32) received a therapeutic dose of E4/DRSP (15/3 mg) once daily for 10 days followed by a supratherapeutic dose (75/15 mg) once daily for another 10 days. Subjects in Group 2 received 400 mg oral moxifloxacin once on Day 1 or once on Day 21 (N = 16 each group). PK determination was performed predose and at 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, and 24 hours postdose on Days 1, 10, 20, and 21. Plasma samples were analyzed for E4/DRSP on Days 10 and 20 in Group 1 and for moxifloxacin on Day 1 (Group 2A) or Day 21 (Group 2B). The analysis of E4/DRSP in plasma samples was performed using validated LC-MS/MS methods. Bioanalytical report is MTA243UF-182432-A for E4 and MTA243UF-182432-B for DRSP. Method validation report is MHUEC302-161697-B (+amendment) for E4, and MHUEC302-161697-E (+amendment) for DRSP.
L1722: ## Group 1:
L1723: - Day 1: One over-encapsulated tablet of 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP and 1 placebo capsule matching over-encapsulated moxifloxacin, administered in the morning
- Day 2 to 10: One over-encapsulated tablet of 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP, administered in the morning
- Day 11 to 20: 75 mg E4/15 mg DRSP (i.e. five over-encapsulated tablets of 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP), administered in the morning
- Day 21: One placebo capsule matching over-encapsulated moxifloxacin, administered in the morning
L1724: ## Group 2A:
L1725: - Day 1: One placebo over-encapsulated tablet matching over-encapsulated E4/DRSP and 1 over-encapsulated tablet of 400 mg moxifloxacin, administered in the morning
- Day 2 to 10: One placebo capsule matching over-encapsulated E4/DRSP, administered in the morning
- Day 11 to 20: Five placebo capsules matching over-encapsulated E4/DRSP, administered in the morning
- Day 21: One placebo capsule matching over-encapsulated moxifloxacin, administered in the morning
L1726: ## Group 2B:
L1727: - Day 1: One placebo capsule matching over-encapsulated E4/DRSP and 1 placebo capsule matching over-encapsulated moxifloxacin, administered in the morning
- Day 2 to 10: One placebo capsule matching over-encapsulated E4/DRSP, administered in the morning
- Day 11 to 20: Five placebo capsules matching over-encapsulated E4/DRSP, administered in the morning
- Day 21: One over-encapsulated tablet of 400 mg moxifloxacin, administered in the morning
L1728: ## Results:
L1729: E4 plasma concentrations following multiple dose administrations of E4/DRSP 15/3 mg and 75/15 mg are presented in **Figure 1**. E4 was rapidly absorbed following oral dosing with a median T_{max} of 1 hour on both Day 10 after therapeutic treatment and Day 20 after supratherapeutic treatment. A 5-fold increase in the amount of E4 administered between Day 10 and Day 20 resulted in a 5.4-fold increase in C_{max} and a 4.5-fold increase in AUC_{0-tlast} from Day 10 (therapeutic) to Day 20 (supratherapeutic) (**Table 1**). DRSP was also rapidly absorbed after oral administration with a median T_{max} of 1.55 hours on Day 10 after therapeutic treatment and 2 hours on Day 20 after supratherapeutic treatment. A 5-fold increase in the amount of DRSP administered between Day 10 and Day 20 resulted in a 5.4-fold increase in C_{max} and a 5.8-fold increase in AUC_{0-tlast} between Day 10 (therapeutic) to Day 20 (supratherapeutic).

L1737: ## Safety:
L1738: A total of 183 TEAEs were reported by 47 (73.4%) subjects.
L1739: - The system organ classes (SOCs) with the greatest number of reported events were gastrointestinal disorders (31 subjects, 48.4%), nervous system disorders (27 subjects, 42.2%), and reproductive system and breast disorders (10 subjects, 15.6%).
- Of the 47 subjects who reported TEAEs, 26 (40.6%) subjects had TEAEs that were considered by the investigator to be likely related to study drug, and 1 (1.6%) subject had a TEAE that was considered by the investigator to be definitely related to study drug.
- Two subjects each experienced an SAEs (complicated migraine which required hospitalization and deep vein thrombosis which was considered an important medical event); 1 of these subjects also had study drug withdrawn due to the AE (complicated migraine). An additional subject who received placebo had study drug withdrawn due to a TEAE of elevated liver enzymes. No deaths were reported.
L1740: **Reviewer's Notes:** *Per the IRT-QT Review by Dr. Girish Bende (DARRTS on August 31, 2020), the therapeutic (15/3 mg) and supratherapeutic (75/15 mg) E4/DRSP doses did not lead to significant QTc interval prolongations.*
L1741: ## 7. Combined Review for In vitro Studies
L1742: E4 displays moderate binding to human plasma protein (45.4% and 50.4%) in female human plasma. E4 does not bind to human sex hormone binding globulin (SHBG). E4 equally distributes between plasma and blood cells in human blood. E4 is extensively metabolized by human hepatocytes *in vitro*. The two main human metabolites were identified as E4-3-glucuronide and E4-16-glucuronide, with E4-16-glucuronide as the quantitatively most important. CYP450 enzymes do not appear to play a major role in the *in vitro* hepatic metabolism of E4. UGT2B7 is the dominant Uridine 5'-diphospho-glucuronosyltransferase (UGT) isoform that catalyzes the biotransformation of E4 into a direct glucuronide and SULT1E1 is the dominant sulfotransferase (SULT) isoform catalyzing E4 sulfation. E4 does not inhibit the major CYP450 isoforms, does not induce CYP1A2, 2B6 and 3A4, and does not suggest a clinically relevant inhibition of UGT. Bidirectional transport studies indicate that E4 is a medium to high permeability compound. E4 is a potential substrate for both efflux ABC transporters P-gp and BCRP but does not inhibit these transporters. While it cannot be entirely excluded that E4 acts as a substrate for OAT3, the importance of this transporter in the cellular uptake of E4 is considered to be low.
L1743: ## Distribution
L1744: ## **Study ES-T33**
L1745: **Title:** *In vitro* binding of estetrol to plasma proteins by equilibrium dialysis
L1746: **Method:** The *in vitro* plasma protein binding (from mouse, rat, monkey and human) was measured and compared using equilibrium dialysis. Specifically, plasma samples from females of each selected species were spiked at the test item concentrations of 1, 10, 100 and 1000 ng/mL for mouse, rat, and monkey and 1, 5, 10 and 50 ng/mL for human. Then, each spiked plasma was dialyzed against PBS solution (pH 7.4) at 37°C in a multi-dialysis system. Membranes with a "cut-off" of 5000 Daltons and a dialysis time of 6 hours were used for all species/concentrations. The time of equilibrium was determined to be between 3 and 6 hours.
L1747: **Result:** The binding of E4 to plasma proteins in samples from women was 47.8\% \pm 1.4\%, 45.5\% \pm 0.4\%, 45.4\% \pm 2.6\% and 50.4\% \pm 0.8\% at 1, 5, 10 and 50 ng/mL, respectively; and there was no indication of a concentration-relationship for any of the evaluated species.
L1748: **Conclusion:** The protein binding of E4 ranged between 45.4% and 50.4% in human plasma
L1749: ## Study PR3025
L1750: **Title:** Relative Binding Affinity of Estetrol to Human Sex Hormone-binding Globulin
L1751: **Method:** An established competitive steroid-binding assay was used to determine the relative binding affinity of estetrol for human SHBG.

L1759: **Conclusion:** E4 does not binding to SHBG.
L1760: ## Study 0031-104
L1761: **Title:** Estetrol: In vitro plasma/blood cell partitioning in human
L1762: **Method:** Whole blood samples from female human were pooled and spiked with ^{14}C-Estetrol at nominal concentrations of 0.1 and 1 \muM and were incubated at 37°C on a rotary mixer for 0, 10, 30, 60 and 120 minutes. After incubation, a packed cell volume (haematocrit) determination was made and duplicate aliquots of blood were analyzed for radioactivity. The remainder of each blood sample was centrifuged. Duplicate aliquots of plasma were analyzed for radioactivity using liquid scintillation counting, and these data were used to determine the blood:plasma ratio and the amount of whole blood radioactivity associated with blood cells.
L1763: **Result:** The blood:plasma ratio and mean value for % radioactivity associated with blood cells at 120 minutes for human are summarized below:
L1764: 0.1 \muM	1 \muM
Blood:plasma ratio	1.21	1.18
% radioactivity associated with blood cells	49.3	48.6
L1765: Data expressed as mean of duplicate determinations
L1766: **Conclusion:** ^{14}\text{C}-E4 is approximately equally distributed between blood cells and plasma. The distribution is not time/concentration dependent.
L1767: ## Metabolism
L1768: ## **Study PR3041**
L1769: **Title:** Species comparison of the metabolite profile of estetrol using rat and human primary hepatocytes in vitro
L1770: **Method:** Freshly isolated (one female rat) or cryopreserved (human; one female donor) hepatocytes in suspension were incubated with estetrol at 1 \mu\text{M} or and 10 \mu\text{M} in a 4-hour time-course study. Unchanged parent compound and metabolites were detected, characterized and profiled using liquid chromatography/mass spectrometry (LC/MS) analysis.
L1771: **Result:** The mean percentage of unchanged estetrol remaining in the hepatocyte incubations at the end of the 4-hour incubation period was 27% and 49% in human, at initial substrate concentrations of 1 \mu\text{M} and 10 \mu\text{M}, respectively. Estetrol was metabolized to at least five metabolites (M1 to M5). The following metabolic pathways have been proposed to account for the various putative metabolites observed: methylation, hydroxylation, dehydroxylation, glucuronidation and sulfation. Alternative pathways that could not be ruled out included dehydrogenation, dihydroxylation and disulfation.
L1772: **Conclusion:** E4 was extensively metabolized into glucuronide and sulfate conjugates by human cryopreserved hepatocytes.
L1773: ## **Study ES-T06**
L1774: **Title:** E4: Metabolism in cryopreserved hepatocytes from mouse, rat, rabbit, monkey and human
L1775: **Method:** E4 was incubated with mouse, rat, rabbit and monkey cryopreserved hepatocytes at a concentration of 10 \mu\text{M} and with human cryopreserved hepatocytes at a concentration of 1 \mu\text{M}. Samples were removed after 0, 1, 2 and 4 hours and quenched with acetonitrile. All samples incubated with E4 were analyzed via LC-MS and all major metabolites were characterized. Each batch of hepatocytes was also incubated with [^{14}\text{C}]7-ethoxycoumarin as a positive control.
L1776: **Result:** Analysis of the samples from all species identified 13 metabolites of E4. Metabolites corresponded to conjugation reactions of E4, with glucuronidation and sulphation being predominant. Only two metabolites were identified following incubation with human cryopreserved hepatocytes and these metabolites corresponded to direct glucuronidation and direct sulphation of E4. These metabolites were also formed by the other species. For metabolite M1, the site of glucuronide conjugation was identified as being at the aromatic hydroxyl group. For the remaining metabolites, identification of the specific sites of modification was not possible due to low levels of metabolites present in the incubation.
L1777: **Conclusion:** Glucuronide and sulphate conjugates are the predominant metabolites of E4.
L1778: ## Study 0031-102
L1779: **Title:** Estetrol: Identification of the major drug metabolizing enzymes (CYP450) involved in human hepatic metabolism in vitro (CYP450 Reaction Phenotyping)
L1780: ## **Method:**
L1781: - ^{14}\text{C}-Estetrol was incubated at a final incubation concentration of 3, 5 and 10 \mu\text{M} with pooled human liver microsomes at protein concentrations of 0.1, 0.25, 0.5 and 1 \text{mg}/\text{mL} for 60 minutes and at a protein concentrations of 0.25 \text{mg}/\text{mL} for 0, 10, 20, 30, 45 and 60 minutes at 37^\circ\text{C} and in the presence of uridine diphosphoglucuronic acid (UDPGA, final concentration of 2 \text{mM}) to determine optimal incubation conditions.
- ^{14}\text{C}-Estetrol was incubated at a final concentration of 5 \mu\text{M} with recombinant cDNA expressed CYPs 1A2, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6 and 3A4 (125 \text{pmol}/\text{mL}) in the presence and absence of NADPH (1 \text{mM}) for 0 and 15 minutes.
L1782: ## **Result:**
L1783: - Following the incubation of ^{14}\text{C}-Estetrol with pooled human liver microsomes in the presence of NADPH, no components other than parent estetrol were detected using flow-through radio-HPLC analysis
- A small amount of a single component (M1) was found in incubations with recombinant cDNA expressed CYP3A4. Considering minimal amounts of M1 were detected in all CYPs incubations including no cofactor controls, M1 may not be metabolically derived.
L1784: **Conclusion:** CYP450 enzymes are not of importance in the in vitro hepatic metabolism of estetrol in human liver.
L1785: ## Study 0031-106
L1786: **Title:** Estetrol: Identification of the major UGT drug metabolizing enzymes involved in human hepatic metabolism in vitro (UGT Reaction Phenotyping)
L1787: ## **Method:**
L1788: - ^{14}\text{C}-Estetrol was incubated at a final incubation concentration of 3, 5 and 10 \mu\text{M} with pooled human liver microsomes at protein concentrations of 0.1, 0.25, 0.5 and 1 \text{mg}/\text{mL} for 60 minutes and at a protein concentrations of 0.25 \text{mg}/\text{mL} for 0, 10, 20, 30, 45 and 60 minutes at 37^\circ\text{C} and in the presence of uridine diphosphoglucuronic acid (UDPGA, final concentration of 2 \text{mM}) to determine optimal incubation conditions. Correlation analysis was then performed using five hepatic UGT-selective activities (UGT1A1, 1A4, 1A6, 1A9 and 2B7) using liver microsomes prepared from single human donor livers (n = 16).
- ^{14}\text{C}-Estetrol was incubated at a final concentration of 10 \mu\text{M} with recombinant cDNA expressed human rUGT enzymes (UGTs 1A1, 1A3, 1A4, 1A6, 1A7, 1A8, 1A9, 1A10, 2B4, 2B7, 2B10, 2B15 and 2B17)
L1789: ## **Result:**
L1790: - Metabolism of estetrol and formation of M1 correlated with the activity of UGT2B7 enzyme (R^2 = 0.6438). There was also a weaker correlation of estetrol metabolism with UGT1A4 (R^2 = 0.4260).
- Only UGT2B7 can catalyze the formation of M1 in vitro. This enzyme is present primarily in liver. UGT1A1, 1A3, 1A4, 1A6, 1A7, 1A8, 1A9, 1A10, 2B4, 2B10, 2B15 and 2B17 did not metabolize estetrol to M1
L1791: ## **Conclusion:**
L1792: UGT2B7 appears to be the dominant form in metabolizing estetrol into a direct glucuronide in human liver.
L1793: ## Study Es0001-NC-011
L1794: **Title:** Estetrol: Identification of the location of the glucuronide substituent in the glucuronide-estetrol formed after incubation of Estetrol with UDP-Glucuronosyltransferase 2B7 (UGT2B7)
L1795: **Method:** [3-14C]Estetrol at 10 µM was incubated with microsomes from cells genetically engineered to express UGT2B7, UDPGA and potassium phosphate buffer, for up to 60 min at 37°C. Incubation samples were analyzed by HPLC with on-line radiodetection.
L1796: **Result:** Incubations of [3-14C]Estetrol with human UGT2B7 produced a single component in the presence of cofactor (UDPGA), labelled as M1. M1 had a similar retention time to that of estetrol C-16 glucuronide reference standard.
L1797: **Conclusion:** UGT2B7 is the dominant UDP-glucuronosyltransferase isoform that catalyzes formation of E-16-glucuronide in vitro.
L1798: ## Study PR3099
L1799: **Title:** Sulfation of Estetrol by Human Cytosolic Sulfotransferases
L1800: **Method:** Human SULT1A1, 1A3, 1B1, 1E1, 1C2, 2A1, or 2B1b, expressed in *Escherichia coli* and purified from bacterial cytosol, were incubated with E4 at 37°C, pH 7.4 in the presence of the co-factor 3'-phosphoadenosine 5'-phospho[35S]sulfate.
L1801: **Result:** Sulfotransferases SULT1A3, 1B1 and 1C2 did not conjugate E4. A single conjugate was observed in incubations with SULT1A1, 1E1, 2A1, and 2B1b, which was tentatively identified as E4-3-sulfate. The isoform with the lowest Km (Michaelis constant: 0.54 µM) and highest Vmax (41 nmol/min/mg) for formation of E4-3- sulfate was SULT1E1. This Km was approximately 13.5-fold higher than the Km of SULT1E1 for E1 (estrone) or E2. The Km for formation of E4-3-sulfate by SULT1A1, the main sulfotransferase in human liver, was 15 µM. E1 and E3 showed stronger inhibition of E2 sulfation by SULT1E1 than E4.
L1802: **Conclusion:** SULT1E1 is the dominant sulfotransferase isoform that catalyzes formation of a direct sulfate (likely to be E4-3-sulfate) in vitro.
L1803: ## Study 0031-100
L1804: **Title:** Estetrol: Assessment of the potential to induce human hepatic cytochrome P450 (CYP) enzymes using human hepatocytes in culture
L1805: **Method:** The potential of E4 (0.01-25 µM) to induce CYP450 enzymes was evaluated in human cryopreserved hepatocytes from three female donors. CYP isoforms evaluated included CYP1A2, 2B6 and 3A4. Omeprazole (50 µM; CYP1A2), phenobarbital (1 mM; CYP2B6) and rifampicin (25 µM; CYP3A4) served as positive controls for induction, while flumazenil served as a negative control.
L1806: ## **Result:**
L1807: Positive controls omeprazole, phenobarbital and rifampicin caused an at least 22-, 31- and 6.6-fold induction of CYP1A2, 2B6 and 3A4 mRNAs, respectively. Flumazenil was negative for induction of
L1808: the three isoforms. The maximum mean-fold change and the concentration (\muM) of E4 causing this maximum effect for the three donors is shown in Table below:
L1809: CYP450	1A2	2B6	3A4
Donor 1	1.17 (E4 0.1 \muM)	2.10 (25 \muM E4)	0.934 (E4 10 \muM)
Donor 2	2.09 (20 \muM E4)	2.45 (E4 20 \muM)	2.16 (E4 25 \muM)
Donor 3	0.823 (0.01 \muM E4))	1.13 (E4 25 \muM)	0.663 (E4 10 \muM)
L1810: These increases were not dose-dependent and/or less than 20% of the increase observed with the respective positive control.
L1811: **Conclusion:** E4 (up to 25 \muM for 72 hours) does not induce CYP450 1A2, 2B6 or 3A4 in human hepatocytes.
L1812: ## Study 0031-101
L1813: **Title:** Estetrol: Assessment of the potential to inhibit human hepatic CYP450 enzymes in vitro
L1814: **Method:** Pooled human liver microsomes were incubated with CYP isoform selective substrates in the presence of 1 mM NADPH. CYP isoforms evaluated included CYP1A2, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, 2E1 and 3A4 (two substrates: testosterone and midazolam). E4 was tested at concentrations of 0, 0.01, 0.1, 1, 10, 30 or 100 \muM for all CYP isoforms, except for CYP3A4 with testosterone as a substrate where the highest concentration tested was 30 \muM. Positive control incubations containing known chemical inhibitors of CYP450 enzymes were conducted in parallel to confirm the suitability of the in vitro systems used.
L1815: ## Result:
L1816: Positive control inhibitors included for each CYP isoform displayed the anticipated reversible or time-dependent inhibitory effects. A summary of the results with E4 is presented in Table below. IC_{50} was &gt;100 \muM for all tested CYP isoforms (including CYP3A4 with midazolam as substrate), except for CYP3A4 with testosterone as substrate. Time-dependent inhibition was not indicated as none of the IC_{50} values determined following a 30-minute pre-incubation of E4 with cofactor was below 50% of the IC_{50} determined without preincubation.

L1826: ## Study 0031-105
L1827: **Title:** Estetrol: Assessment of the potential to inhibit UGT enzymes in vitro
L1828: **Method:** The inhibitory effect of E4 (0.1-100 \muM) on UGT isoforms was evaluated using pooled human liver microsomes (n=150, mixed gender) and/or human recombinant UGT (rUGT) Supersomes. Pooled human liver microsomes were incubated with selective substrates for UGT isoforms 1A1, 1A4, 1A6, 1A9, 2B7 or 2B17, in the presence of 10 mM UDPGA. Human UGT1A1, 1A3, 1A4, 1A6, 1A7, 1A8, 1A9, 1A10, 2B4, 2B7, 2B10, 2B15, or 2B17 Supersomes were incubated with appropriate substrates, in the presence of 10 mM UDPGA. E4 was tested at concentrations of 0, 0.1, 1, 10, 30 or 100 \muM for all UGT isoforms. For each of the UGT isoforms, an appropriate reference inhibitor was included as a positive control for inhibition which displayed the anticipated inhibitory effects.
L1829: **Result:** A summary of the inhibitory results obtained in the study is presented in Table below. At the highest concentration of 100 \muM, E4 showed less than 50% inhibition of all investigated UGT isoforms except UGT1A9 and UGT2B7. For these two isoforms low inhibitory properties were reported; IC_{50} values were 95.5 \muM for UGT1A9 in human liver microsomes and 25.8 \muM in human Supersomes, respectively. For UGT2B7, respective IC_{50} values in human liver microsomes and Supersomes were 89.4 and 38.0 \muM, respectively.
