L1837: ## Study 0031- NC017 (Bidirectional transport study in human Caco-2 cells)
L1838: **Title:** Study of the solubility and the in vitro permeability of E4
L1839: **Method:** E4 (2, 10, 50 and 200 \muM) were added to either the apical or the basolateral compartment (donor compartment) of human Caco-2 cell monolayer cultures and incubated for up to 120 minutes. Samples collection in apical and basal compartments during 2 hours after addition of the test compounds in the apical or basolateral chamber. At different time-points, sampling, filtering and assay of the samples
L1840: using a LC-MS/MS method after the derivatization reaction.
L1841: **Result:** Controls for cell performance consisted of propranolol permeability, and efflux ratios of [^{3}\text{H}]-digoxin (P-gp), [^{3}\text{H}]-prazosin (breast cancer resistance protein, BCRP) and [^{14}\text{C}]-methotrexate (MRP2). TEER (&gt; 300 \text{ Ohm}\cdot\text{cm}^2) and Lucifer Yellow permeability (2.25 \times 10^{-6} \text{ cm/sec}) showed acceptable barrier integrity. The overall average apical to basolateral permeability is 1.8 \times 10^{-5} \text{ cm/sec}, indicating a high cellular permeability. The overall average efflux ratio was 3.19 (i.e. &gt; 2), suggesting E4 was possibly a substrate for an efflux transporter.
L1842: ## **Conclusion:**
L1843: E4 is a medium to high permeability compound and may be a substrate for efflux transporters.
L1844: ## **Study 0031-103 (Interaction of E4 as a substrate or an inhibitor of human ABC efflux transporters)**
L1845: **Title:** Estetrol: Assessment of efflux-mediated transport
L1846: ## **Method:**
L1847: - ABC transporter substrate assessment: Estetrol was incubated (in triplicate) with MDCKII cell lines transfected with either the human P-gp or BCRP gene, as well as wild-type (WT; non-transfected) cells at four concentrations, of 0.1, 1, 10 and 30 \mu\text{M}. Samples were taken from the corresponding receiver compartment (basolateral or apical, respectively) after 30, 45 or 60 minutes of incubation
- ABC transporter inhibitor assessment: an appropriate radio-labeled probe substrate and 0, 0.1, 1, 10 or 30 \mu\text{M} E4 were added to either the apical or the basolateral compartment (donor compartment) of the cell monolayer cultures. Samples were taken from the corresponding receiver compartment (basolateral or apical, respectively) after 30, 45 or 60 minutes of incubation. The efflux ratio of probe substrate was calculated as (Papp (B-A)) / (Papp (A-B)).
L1848: ## **Result:**
L1849: E4 displayed an overall average apical to basolateral permeability of &gt; 4 \times 10^{-6} \text{ cm/sec}, which classified E4 as a medium to high permeability compound.
L1850: - E4 had no impact on the net efflux ratios of the probe substrates of P-gp and BCRP. E4 was therefore not an inhibitor of these transporters.
- The net efflux ratio for E4 was &gt; 6 at all time-points where calculable. These net efflux ratios were reduced to \le 1.2 in the presence of the BCRP inhibitor Ko143 (where Papp was fully quantifiable). These data indicated that E4 acts as a substrate of BCRP. In the substrate identification assay with P-gp, the data set at 60 minutes of incubation was incomplete, as E4 concentrations in the receiver compartments were above the limit of quantification at 1, 10 and 30 \mu\text{M}. At 30 and 45 minutes of incubation, net efflux ratios at 1, 10 and 30 (30 minutes only) \mu\text{M} were &gt; 2, but inhibition of these ratios by verapamil was not consistently observed.
L1851: ## **Conclusion:**
L1852: E4 does not inhibit P-gp or BCRP. E4 is a substrate of BCRP. The substrate status for P-gp is not conclusive.
L1853: ## **Study Es0001-NC-013**
L1854: **Title:** Estetrol: P-Glycoprotein (P-gp) Substrate Interactions
L1855: ## **Method:**
L1856: Estetrol was incubated (in triplicate) with P-gp transfected and WT MDCKII cell lines at four concentrations, of 1.9, 19, 60 and 190 \mu\text{M}. Estetrol was dosed to apical (A) or basolateral (B) well
L1857: compartments in the presence and absence of the reference inhibitor, verapamil (100 \muM). Prior to initiation of the experiment, triplicate samples from the corresponding dosing solutions were sampled as a measure of the time zero concentration. The transport experiment was initiated by reconstruction of the receiver and donor compartments into one plate following dosing and t=0 sampling. Subsequent samples were taken from the donor compartment at 120 minutes and from the receiver compartment at 60, 90 and 120 minutes. The experiment was terminated after 120 minutes by the separation of the receiver and donor compartments.
L1858: **Result:** Estetrol displayed medium permeability (A - B Papp 3.35 - 4.88 \times 10^{-6} cm/s and 2.43 - 3.40 \times 10^{-6} cm/s) in MDCKII-WT and MDCKII-MDR1 cells respectively, across all concentrations tested, with good recovery (&gt;70%) observed in all incubation samples. Estetrol is likely to be a substrate of P-gp, as the net efflux ratio (NER) was \ge 2 at all concentrations and timepoints examined. Further, these net efflux ratios were inhibited by &gt;50% by verapamil, with the exception of that of 19 \muM estetrol (60 minutes), 60 \muM estetrol (90 minutes) and 190 \muM estetrol (120 minutes), where inhibition was 45 - 49%. E4 displayed an overall average apical to basolateral permeability of &gt; 4 \times 10^{-6} cm/sec, which classified E4 as a medium to high permeability compound.
L1859: ## Conclusion:
L1860: Estetrol is likely a substrate of P-gp, as net efflux ratios were \ge 2 and were reduced by 45 - 62%, in the presence of the known P-gp inhibitor, verapamil.
L1861: ## Study 0031-107
L1862: **Title:** Estetrol: Assessment of uptake transporter interactions
L1863: ## Method:
L1864: - SLC transporter inhibition assessment: Estetrol (0, 0.1, 1, 10 and 30 \muM) was assessed as a potential inhibitor of OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2-K in incubations with HEK293 cells transiently transfected with one SLC transporter and wild-type controls. Incubations with probe substrate in the absence or presence of an appropriate reference inhibitor served as a positive control. E4 was designated as a potential inhibitor if the uptake ratio (uptake rate transfected / uptake rate wild-type) of probe substrate was \ge 2 and if this ratio was decreased by E4.
- SLC transporter substrate assessment: Estetrol (0.1, 1, 10 and 30 \muM) was assessed as a potential substrate of OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2-K in incubations with HEK293 cells transiently transfected with one SLC transporter and wild-type (WT) controls. E4 was designated as a potential substrate if it displayed an uptake ratio \ge 2 and if this ratio was reduced &gt; 50% by reference inhibitor.
L1865: ## Result:
L1866: - Transporters OATP1B3, OAT1, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2-K were not inhibited by E4. For OATP1B1, the extent of inhibition (46.6%) at the highest evaluated concentration of 30 \muM was less than 50% which was considered insignificant and thus an IC_{50} was not calculated. E4 inhibited the transporter OAT3 with an IC_{50} of 16.5 \muM.
- Transporters OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2-K did not play a role in the cellular uptake of E4 (uptake ratio in transfected versus wild-type cells &lt; 2 at all E4 concentrations). For transporter OAT3, an uptake ratio of 3.09 was noted at a concentration of 1 \muM E4, but the uptake ratio was below 2 at 0.1 \muM (1.53), 10 \muM (1.95) and 30 \muM (1.95). Uptake rate-versus-E4 concentration plots in OAT3-transfected cells indicated that E4 transport was not saturated over the concentration range investigated and a small deviation from linearity suggested that either the 0.1 \muM or the 1 \muM value was an outlier. The average uptake ratio (i.e. 2.14) at four concentrations was close
L1867: to 2. Exclusion of the 1 µM data (uptake ratio furthest from the mean uptake ratio) would lead to the conclusion that E4 is not a substrate for OAT3.
L1868: ## **Conclusion:**
L1869: E4 does not inhibit SLC transporters OATP1B3, OAT1, OCT2, MATE1 and MATE2-K, but inhibit OAT3 with IC50 of 16.5 µM.
L1870: **Reviewer's Comment:** Based on FDA *in vitro* DDI Guidance, the calculated values for R1 and R1, gut are less than 1.1 and 11, respectively. Therefore, E4 is unlikely to inhibit OAT3 at the clinically relevant dose.
L1871: ## 8. Population PK and/or PD Analyses
L1872: ## 8.1 Population PK (popPK) Analyses
L1873: ## Review Summary
L1874: The Applicant submitted a population report entitled “Pharmacokinetic Modeling of Drospirenone and Estetrol”. In general, the Applicant’s population PK analysis for drospirenone is considered acceptable with limitations, but the population PK analysis for estetrol is inadequate and inconclusive. The results from these popPK analyses were not used to support any labeling language or exposure-response analysis.
L1875: **Objectives:** The objectives of the report were to assess the effect of various individual characteristics/covariates (e.g., body weight, race, smoking, and fed versus fasted status) on the pharmacokinetics (PK) of estetrol and drospirenone.
L1876: **Data:** The population pharmacokinetic analysis included PK data from healthy volunteers enrolled in three clinical studies: Es0001-C101, MIT-Es0001-C103, and MIT-Es0001-C302. Dosing regimens and PK data collections are summarized in **Table 1**.
L1877: **Table 1: Studies included for population PK analysis**

L1894: ## Population PK Model for Drospirenone
L1895: **Base Model:** The selected base model for drospirenone was a two-compartment model with first-order absorption with a lag time for absorption. Relative bioavailability (F1) was fixed to 1 and an interindividual variability (IIV) on F1 was estimated. IIV was estimated for CL/F, Vc/F, Ka and F1. A negative covariance was estimated between IIV on CL/F and IIV on Vc/F. An inter-occasion variability (IOV) was estimated on F1. The residual (unexplained) variability was modeled using a combined additive and proportional error model.
L1896: **Figure 1. Schematic for the Final Drospirenone Structural PK Model**
L1897: Source: Figure 1 on page 22 of Applicant's population PK report
L1898: **Final Model:** Covariate analysis were conducted with forward addition and backward elimination. The effects of body weight, race, smoking status, diet, study, dose, and race were tested on volume of distribution, clearance, Ka, and F1.
L1899: The final drospirenone model was a two-compartment model with first-order absorption with a lag time for absorption. An IIV was estimated on CL/F, Vc/F and F1 (Figure 1). A negative covariance was estimated between IIV on CL/F and IIV on Vc/F. An IOV was estimated on F1. Residual variability was modeled by a combined additive and proportional error model. The CL/F and volumes were scaled allometrically as depicted in Equation 3, Equation 4, and Equation 5. Fasted/fed status was found to be a significant covariate on Ka as outlines in section 3.3.2 and in Equation 6.
L1900: Equation 3. TVCL/F = THETA(1) * \left(\frac{Weight}{Median Weight}\right)^{0.75}
L1901: Equation 4. TVVc/F = THETA(2) * \left(\frac{Weight}{Median Weight}\right)^{1}
L1902: Equation 5. TVVp/F = THETA(3) * \left(\frac{Weight}{Median Weight}\right)^{1}
L1903: The parameter estimates from the final model including covariate effects are summarized in **Table 3**.
L1904: **Table 3: PopPK Parameter Estimates from the Final Drospirenone Model**

L1908: ## Model Evaluation
L1909: The final model was evaluated graphically by goodness-of-fit plots, visual predictive checks (VPCs) as well as bootstrap evaluation. The goodness-of-fit plots for the final model are displayed in **Figure 2** and the VPCs plots are demonstrated in **Figure 3**, respectively.
L1910: **Figure 2: Goodness-of-Fit Plots for the Final Drospirenone PopPK Model**
L1911: Upper left is the “typical patient” predicted value vs the observed value, upper right is “individualized” parameter predictions vs observed. Lower left is the time after dose vs the individual weighted residual (a measure of how well predicted the observations are). Lower right is the predicted value vs the individual weighted residual
L1912: Source: Figure 2 on page 24 of Applicant's population PK report
L1913: Figure 3: VPC by dose for the Final Drospirenone PopPK Model, 0 to 200 hours
L1914: *Note:* VPC consists of simulating the trial ~ 1000 times (same patients, same doses, same sampling times), then comparing the range that the model predicts, assuming the model is “correct” with the observed data. Solid red line is the median of observed data, dashed red lines are 90% interval for observed data. Solid black line is the median of simulated predictions, dashed black lines are 90% interval for simulated predictions. Red blocks are 95% confidence interval for median of simulated predictions. Blue blocks are 95% confidence interval of the simulation predicted data. If the model is “correct”, the observed data should lie near the center of the simulation predicted data.
L1915: *Source:* Figure 8 on page 30 of Applicant's population PK report
L1916: **Effect of Covariates on Drospirenone PK Parameters:** The secondary pharmacokinetic parameters for drospirenone are given in Table 4. The secondary pharmacokinetic parameters for drospirenone in Table 4 are representative of a subject with a median body weight of 66.7 kg in a fasted and a fed state. Over the weight range of 42 kg to 117 kg, the CL/F value is predicted to have of range of 3.59 L/h to 7.74 L/h, Vc/F will have a range of 53.3 L to 147 L and Vp/F will have a range of 134 L to 372 L. The fasted/fed status proved to be a significant covariate and was retained in the final drospirenone PopPK model. A higher Ka (faster absorption) was estimated for fasted status as compared to fed status, which is consistent with results in the food effect study Es0001-C101.
L1917: **Table 4: Secondary PK Parameters for 3 mg Drospirenone**

L1919: *Source:* Table 4 on page 46 of Applicant's population PK report
L1920: **Effect of Covariates on Estetrol Ctrough:** The effect of covariates on estetrol cannot be estimated with the population PK analysis as the model was inadequate and inclusive. A linear mixed ANCOVA analysis was conducted by the Applicant to evaluate the effect of body weight, race, and smoking status on Ctrough of estetrol. Ctrough was defined in two sets of data (\ge 20 hours and \le 28 hours after the last dose and \ge 18 hours and \le 30 hours after the last dose). Race and smoking status were not identified as significant covariates.
L1921: Ctrough decreases with body weight at E4 doses of 15 mg, 30 and 75 mg, reaching a statistical significance for 15 mg in both sets (p=0.0044 and p=0.0020, respectively). An increase of Ctrough with body weight was observed for the 60-mg E4 dose. This relationship was close to statistical significance in both sets (p=0.069 and p=0.062, respectively), but should nevertheless be cautiously interpreted given the limited data (n=10) available at this dose level.
L1922: *Reviewer's comments:*
L1923: *The Applicant's population PK analysis for estetrol is not adequate and inconclusive as to assessment of the effect of covariates. The Applicant used a two-compartment model to describe the PK profile of*
L1924: *estetrol, however, the model was not able to explain the evident multiple peak phenomenon likely due to enterohepatic circulation. The model cannot identify any covariates that can explain the interindividual variations in key PK parameters. Since the Applicant also acknowledged the flaws and the conclusions from this analysis were not used for labeling or dosing decisions, the reviewer deems this model to be not acceptable and will need further development in the future when more data are available.*
L1925: *The Applicant's population PK analysis for drospirenone is considered acceptable in describing the observed trend of drospirenone PK profile as shown in the goodness-of-fits plots. However, the predictive performance of the model was not adequate as indicated in the VPC plots. Model-predicted 5th and 95th percentile values are lower and higher than those observed, respectively. Therefore, the usefulness of the model is limited. The model can be used to estimate the PK parameters of drospirenone but can not be used for simulations.*
L1926: *Independent analysis was conducted by the reviewer to assess the appropriateness of the model and evaluate the effect of covariates. The reviewer verified the Applicant's analysis and obtained results that were consistent with those submitted by the Applicant.*

The 5 mg dose (blue line) shows the lowest peak concentration (around 0.5 ng/mL) and fastest elimination. The 15 mg dose (red line) shows a peak concentration around 1.5 ng/mL. The 45 mg dose (green line) shows the highest peak concentration (around 5 ng/mL). All doses exhibit a secondary absorption peak around 24 hours.
L1959: Figure 1: A semi-logarithmic plot showing Mean estetrol serum concentrations (pg/mL) versus Time (hr) for single ascending doses of E4 (1 mg, 10 mg, and 100 mg). The 1 mg dose shows the lowest peak concentration and fastest clearance, while the 100 mg dose shows the highest peak concentration and slowest clearance. All doses show a rapid initial decline followed by a slower elimination phase.
L1960: Figure 1: Arithmetic mean plasma concentrations of E4 from 6 to 72 h post-dose on Day 28 for all dose groups (Study PR3054). The graph shows E4 plasma concentration (pg/mL) on a logarithmic scale versus Time (h). Four dose groups are plotted: 2 mg E4 (black squares), 10 mg E4 (red circles), 20 mg E4 (green asterisks), and 40 mg E4 (blue triangles). All groups show rapid absorption, with Cmax occurring around 12-24 hours. The 40 mg group exhibits the highest Cmax and AUC, followed by the 20 mg group. The 10 mg group shows intermediate concentrations, and the 2 mg group shows the lowest concentrations.
L1961: Bar chart showing the percentage of subjects without scheduled (withdrawal) bleeding across Treatment Cycle 2, Treatment Cycle 3, and Treatment Cycle 6 for four groups: Qlaira, 20 mg E4/3 mg DRSP, 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP, and two E4/LNG groups (20 mg E4/150 µg LNG and 15 mg E4/150 µg LNG). The Y-axis represents the percentage of subjects (0 to 100). In all cycles, the E4/LNG groups show significantly higher percentages of subjects without scheduled bleeding compared to the E4/DRSP groups.
L1962: Bar chart showing the percentage of subjects without scheduled bleeding across Treatment Cycle 2, Treatment Cycle 3, and Treatment Cycle 6 for four groups: Qlaira, 20 mg E4/3 mg DRSP, 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP, and two E4/LNG groups (20 mg E4/150 µg LNG and 15 mg E4/150 µg LNG). The Y-axis represents the percentage of subjects (0 to 40). The E4/LNG groups show significantly higher percentages of subjects without scheduled bleeding compared to the E4/DRSP groups, especially in Cycle 6.
L1963: Stacked bar chart showing Hoogland Scores Results for Cycle 1 and Cycle 3. The Y-axis is Percent of Subjects (0 to 100). The X-axis shows four treatments: 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP (N=40), 20 ug EE/3 mg DRSP [Yaz] (N=41), 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP (N=40), and 20 ug EE/3 mg DRSP [Yaz] (N=41). The legend defines the Hoogland Score categories: 1: NO ACTIVITY, 2: POTENTIAL ACTIVITY, 3: NON-ACTIVE FLS, 4: ACTIVE FLS, 5: LUF, and 6: OVULATION. In Cycle 1, both treatments show high NO ACTIVITY (around 90%) and LUF (around 10%). In Cycle 3, both treatments show high NO ACTIVITY (around 90%) and LUF (around 10%).
L1964: Figure 2 displays four sets of line graphs showing mean hormone concentrations over time (Cycle Days 0 to 27) for two treatment groups: 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP (N=37, blue line) and 20 \mug E4/3 mg DRSP (Yaz) (N=40, red line). The graphs are arranged in a 2x2 grid, comparing Cycle 1 and Cycle 3 for each hormone.
Top Left: FSH (\muU/mL)Cycle 1: FSH levels are generally stable or slightly decreasing, ranging from approximately 4 to 6 \muU/mL.
Cycle 3: FSH levels are generally stable or slightly decreasing, ranging from approximately 4 to 6 \muU/mL.

Top Right: Estradiol (pg/mL)Cycle 1: Estradiol levels fluctuate significantly. The 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP group peaks around Day 9 (approx. 20 pg/mL) and Day 27 (approx. 25 pg/mL). The 20 \mug E4/3 mg DRSP group peaks around Day 9 (approx. 15 pg/mL) and Day 27 (approx. 20 pg/mL).
Cycle 3: Estradiol levels fluctuate significantly. The 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP group peaks around Day 9 (approx. 30 pg/mL) and Day 27 (approx. 25 pg/mL). The 20 \mug E4/3 mg DRSP group peaks around Day 9 (approx. 20 pg/mL) and Day 27 (approx. 20 pg/mL).

Bottom Left: Luteinizing Hormone (mU/mL)Cycle 1: LH levels are generally stable, ranging from approximately 4 to 6 mU/mL.
Cycle 3: LH levels are generally stable, ranging from approximately 4 to 6 mU/mL.

Bottom Right: Progesterone (nmol/L)Cycle 1: Progesterone levels are generally low, fluctuating between 0.5 and 1.5 nmol/L. Both groups show a peak around Day 9 (approx. 1.2 nmol/L).
Cycle 3: Progesterone levels are generally low, fluctuating between 0.5 and 1.5 nmol/L. Both groups show a peak around Day 9 (approx. 1.0 nmol/L).

Legend: 15 mg E4/3 mg DRSP (N=37) and 20 \mug E4/3 mg DRSP (Yaz) (N=40).
L1965: Figure 1: Arithmetic mean (±SD) plasma PK E4 concentration up to 12 hours following single oral doses of E4/DRSP 15/3 mg administered alone (Treatment A) or in combination with valproic acid (Treatment B). The graph shows concentration (pg/mL) versus time point (h). Treatment B (E4/DRSP + VAL) shows higher peak concentration and overall exposure compared to Treatment A (E4/DRSP alone).
L1966: Figure 1: Mean (±SD) E4 plasma concentration vs time profiles following multiple dose administrations of E4/DRSP 15/3 mg and 75/15 mg (Study MIT-Es0001-C106). The graph shows E4 plasma concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis (0 to 120000) versus Time Postdose (hours) on the X-axis (0 to 24). Two profiles are shown: E4/DRSP 15/3 mg (Day 10) and E4/DRSP 75/15 mg (Day 20). Both profiles show a rapid peak concentration around 1 hour postdose. The 75/15 mg profile consistently shows a higher mean concentration and larger standard deviation (SD) bars compared to the 15/3 mg profile, indicating higher exposure.
L1967: Figure 2: Mean (±SD) DRSP plasma concentration vs time profiles following multiple dose administrations of E4/DRSP 15/3 mg and 75/15 mg. The graph shows Mean Plasma Concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis (0 to 300) versus Time Postdose (hours) on the X-axis (0 to 24). Two profiles are shown: E4/DRSP 15/3 mg (open circles, Day 10) and E4/DRSP 75/15 mg (filled circles, Day 20). The 75/15 mg profile shows significantly higher plasma concentrations, peaking around 180 ng/mL, while the 15/3 mg profile peaks around 40 ng/mL.
L1968: Schematic diagram of the Final Drospirenone Structural PK Model. It shows a two-compartment model. The Central Volume (Vc/F) is connected to the Peripheral Volume (Vp/F) via a transfer rate Q/F. The Central Volume (Vc/F) is also connected to clearance CL/F. The absorption rate is governed by Ka and F1.
L1969: Figure 3 displays four plots related to the population PK model validation:
Upper Left: Observed Concentrations (ng/ml) vs Population Predictions (ng/ml). Shows a strong positive correlation, with observed values generally falling within the 95% prediction interval (red line).
Upper Right: Observed Concentrations (ng/ml) vs Individual Predictions (ng/ml). Shows a strong positive correlation, with observed values generally falling within the 95% prediction interval (red line).
Lower Left: Individual Weighted Residuals vs Time after Dose (hours). Residuals are centered around zero, indicating good model fit over time.
Lower Right: Individual Weighted Residuals vs Population Predictions (ng/ml). Residuals are centered around zero, indicating good model fit across predicted concentrations.
L1970: Figure 3 shows four Visual Predictive Checks (VPCs) for Drospirenone concentrations (ng/mL) over time (hours) for doses 3, 6, 12, and 15. The plots show observed concentrations (red dots) and predicted concentrations (black solid line) along with 95% prediction intervals (blue shaded area). The y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 0.1 to 100 ng/mL. The x-axis ranges from 0 to 200 hours. The observed data generally falls within the predicted intervals for all doses.
